<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the asymptotically hyperbolic Penrose inequality for sufficiently small maximal vacuum conformal perturbations of a positive-mass Schwarzschild–anti-de Sitter exterior, for every fixed decaying transverse-traceless seed and every solution branch satisfying the stated decay and mass assumptions. The bound uses the area of the marginally outer trapped boundary itself; it requires neither outermostness nor outer area-minimization, and no bulk-versus-boundary domination condition. For sufficiently small positive parameters, equality holds for radial seeds and the inequality is strict otherwise.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every background, fixed TT seed, and solution branch satisfying <a href="#eq:background" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:background">[eq:background]</a>–<a href="#eq:branch" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:branch">[eq:branch]</a> and the mass assumptions above, there is $0<\epsilon_0\leq\epsilon_*$ such that $$m_{\mathrm{AH}}(\epsilon)\geq \sqrt{\frac{A_\epsilon}{16\pi}}\left(1+\frac{A_\epsilon}{4\pi}\right) \qquad(0\leq\epsilon<\epsilon_0).$$ For all sufficiently small positive $\epsilon$, equality holds if the seed is radial, and the inequality is strict otherwise.
  - Explanation: Establishes the local mass–boundary-area inequality for each admissible fixed seed and branch, with radial equality and nonradial strictness.
- **C2** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-penrose)): In addition to the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, suppose $q_{ss}\geq0$ on $S$. Assume, for every sufficiently small positive $\epsilon$, that no compact smooth embedded two-sided surface in the interior enclosing $S$ has $\theta_+\leq0$ everywhere, and that every smooth enclosing surface has area at least $A_\epsilon$. Disconnected enclosing competitors are allowed, and $S$ itself is allowed in the area infimum. Then the exact local Penrose inequality holds with $A_{\min}(S)=A_\epsilon$.
  - Explanation: Identifies the boundary area with the enclosing-area infimum under the additional boundary sign, outermostness and area hypotheses.
- **C3** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-static)): The tensor $B$ and the static lapse satisfy $$\begin{gathered} B=B_s\,\mathrm{d}s^2+B_t r^2\sigma,\qquad B_s=1-\frac{2m_0}{r^3},\qquad B_t=1+\frac{m_0}{r^3},\label{eq:B-components}\\ \nabla^2N=NB,\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_{g_0}B=3,\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{div}}_{g_0}B=0,\qquad LN=0.\label{eq:static-identities} \end{gathered}$$ Moreover $D$ is positive on all spherical harmonics and $$\label{eq:boundary-constants} b_*(4\pi a^2)=m_0,\qquad b_*'(4\pi a^2)=\frac{\kappa}{8\pi}.$$
  - Explanation: Provides static curvature identities and the positive boundary operator used in the area correction.
- **C4** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-inverse)): There are constants $c_\beta,C_\beta>0$ with the following properties. Suppose $z=o(e^{-\beta s})$ uniformly at infinity and $$(L-V)z=F,\qquad \\|V\\|_{C^0(M)}\leq c_\beta.$$ If either $z\|_S=b$ or $\partial_s z\|_S=b$, then $$\\|z\\|_\beta\leq C_\beta \bigl(\\|F\\|_\beta+\\|b\\|_{C^0(S)}\bigr). \label{ana:comparison}$$ The constants do not depend on an angular cutoff. The same estimate holds for $\partial_s z-a_*z=b$ if $\\|a_*\\|_{C^0(S)}\leq\beta/2$.* *If $F$ and $b$ are smooth and of finite angular type, and $F=O(e^{-\gamma s})$ for some $\gamma>3$, there is a unique decaying finite angular type solution of $Lu=F$ with either of the above Dirichlet or Neumann data. It satisfies $$u=r^{-3}\zeta(x)+o(r^{-3}),\qquad \partial_s\bigl(u-r^{-3}\zeta(x)\bigr)=o(r^{-3}) \label{ana:inverse-asymptotic}$$ for a smooth finite angular type function $\zeta$. These statements hold after any fixed number of angular differentiations. If $\partial_s^jF=O(e^{-\gamma s})$ for $0\leq j\leq k$, the remainder in <a href="#ana:inverse-asymptotic" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ana:inverse-asymptotic">[ana:inverse-asymptotic]</a> can also be differentiated in $s$ through order $k+2$, with each resulting remainder $o(r^{-3})$.
  - Explanation: Controls the scalar inverse in a weighted uniform norm and establishes differentiated asymptotics for finite angular data.
- **C5** (construction, [source](#stmt-ana-coefficients)): For every smooth finite angular type seed in $C^{1,\alpha}_\tau$, the coefficients through degree two are well defined and $$u_j=r^{-3}\zeta_j(x)+o(r^{-3}),\qquad j=1,2.$$ The first coefficient has this asymptotic with arbitrarily many spatial derivatives. The second has it with the first spatial derivatives, in particular with every derivative needed at degree two below.* *For the fourth-order construction, suppose specifically that $$\begin{gathered} q=\mathop{\mathrm{Hess}}v-Bv,\qquad Lv=0,\qquad v\longrightarrow0,\\ v\|_S\text{ has only constant and degree-one spherical modes}. \end{gathered} \label{ana:kernel-seed}$$ Corollary <a href="#cor:kernel" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:kernel">16</a> will show that every seed with zero quadratic deficit has this form, so fourth-order estimates are needed only in this class. Then $q$ and all its derivatives have $O(r^{-3})$ scaled components. The coefficients $u_1,\ldots,u_4$ exist and have the above asymptotic with arbitrarily many spatial derivatives.* *Let $p=2$ in the first case and $p=4$ in the second. In the coordinate $\eta=\operatorname{arsinh}r$, the conformal metric jet $G=(1+\sum_{j=1}^p\epsilon^ju_j)^4g_0$, taken through degree $p$, satisfies $$\begin{gathered} G-b=r^{-3}\bigl(C\,\mathrm{d}\eta^2+E r^2\sigma\bigr)+o(r^{-3}),\\ C=2m_0+4\sum_{j=1}^p\epsilon^j\zeta_j,\qquad E=4\sum_{j=1}^p\epsilon^j\zeta_j. \end{gathered} \label{ana:metric-asymptotic}$$ For coefficient $j$, the remainder has this decay after angular and $\eta$ derivatives through order $p-j+1$, in scaled coframes $\mathrm{d}\eta,r\,\mathrm{d}x$.
  - Explanation: Constructs conformal coefficients with the spatial derivatives required for the quadratic argument and, in the kernel, the quartic argument.
- **C6** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-green)): For every sufficiently small member of the actual branch, $$p_\mu(\epsilon)-p_\mu(0) =\frac1{2\pi}\left\{ \int_S\bigl(\psi\partial_sW_\mu-W_\mu\partial_s\psi\bigr)\,\mathrm{d}A_{g_0} -\int_M\bigl(W_\mu L\psi-\psi LW_\mu\bigr)\,\mathrm{d}V_{g_0} \right\}. \label{ana:green}$$ In particular the right-hand side is an absolutely convergent integral expression. More generally the linear functional $$\mathcal G_\mu(u,F,B) =\frac1{2\pi}\left\{ \int_S(u\partial_sW_\mu-W_\mu B)\,\mathrm{d}A_{g_0} -\int_M(W_\mu F-uLW_\mu)\,\mathrm{d}V_{g_0}\right\} \label{ana:green-functional}$$ is bounded in the norms $\\|u\\|_\beta+\\|F\\|_{2\beta}+\\|B\\|_{C^0(S)}$. For finite angular type coefficient jets, this same functional computes the coefficient of their metric mass.
  - Explanation: Replaces derivative mass fluxes by absolutely convergent integrals before parameter expansion.
- **C7** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-rotation)): Every $q\in\mathscr T_{\tau,\alpha}$ is a limit, in $\\|\cdot\\|_\beta$, of finite angular type tensors $q_\nu\in\mathscr T_{\tau,\alpha}$. The approximants may be chosen as averages of rotated copies of $q$. Their weighted norms are uniformly bounded, and the construction preserves a nonnegative boundary value $q_{ss}\|_S$ when that condition holds.* *The maps $q\mapsto u_1[q]$ and $q\mapsto u_2[q]$ defined for finite angular type seeds extend uniquely by these approximations to continuous, respectively linear and quadratic, maps on $\mathscr T_{\tau,\alpha}$ with values in the weighted uniform norm. The corresponding pairs $(F_j,B_j)$ in <a href="#ana:first-two" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ana:first-two">[ana:first-two]</a> extend continuously in the norms $\\|\cdot\\|_{2\beta}$ and $\\|\cdot\\|_{C^0(S)}$.
  - Explanation: Extends coefficient functionals from finite angular type to arbitrary prescribed decaying TT seeds.
- **C8** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-expansions)): For every prescribed seed and branch in the theorem, set $p=2$ and take $u_j,F_j,B_j$ from Lemma <a href="#lem:rotation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:rotation">7</a>. If the seed has the form <a href="#ana:kernel-seed" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ana:kernel-seed">[ana:kernel-seed]</a>, we may instead take $p=4$ with the finite coefficient construction. In both cases, $$\begin{aligned} \left\\|\psi-\sum_{j=1}^p\epsilon^ju_j\right\\|_\beta &=O(\epsilon^{p+1}),\notag\\ \left\\|L\psi-\sum_{j=1}^p\epsilon^jF_j\right\\|_{2\beta} &=O(\epsilon^{p+1}),\notag\\ \left\\|\partial_s\psi\|_S-\sum_{j=1}^p\epsilon^jB_j\right\\|_{C^0(S)} &=O(\epsilon^{p+1}). \label{ana:three-remainders} \end{aligned}$$ The area and all four mass components have actual expansions to the same order. Their mass coefficients are $$a_{\mu j}[q]=\mathcal G_\mu(u_j,F_j,B_j),\qquad p_\mu(\epsilon)=p_\mu(0)+\sum_{j=1}^p\epsilon^j a_{\mu j}[q] +O(\epsilon^{p+1}). \label{ana:mass-coefficients}$$* *In particular, the actual deficit satisfies $$\mathfrak D_q(\epsilon)=c_2[q]\epsilon^2+O(\epsilon^3) \label{ana:deficit-second}$$ for every seed under consideration. The coefficient $c_2$ is a continuous quadratic form on $\mathscr T_{\tau,\alpha}$ in the $\\|\cdot\\|_\beta$ topology, invariant under rotations. For seeds of the form <a href="#ana:kernel-seed" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ana:kernel-seed">[ana:kernel-seed]</a> there is additionally the actual expansion $$\mathfrak D_q(\epsilon) =c_2[q]\epsilon^2+c_3[q]\epsilon^3+c_4[q]\epsilon^4+O(\epsilon^5). \label{ana:deficit-fourth}$$ The subsequent geometric arguments determine the signs and the vanishing of these coefficients.
  - Explanation: Matches finite coefficient calculations with the actual branch and its mass and area remainders.
- **C9** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-ana-order-of-limits)): The derivative control used in <a href="#ana:flux-errors" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ana:flux-errors">[ana:flux-errors]</a> comes from each actual member’s assumed $C^{2,\alpha}_\tau$ decay. It is not a consequence of the weighted uniform Taylor estimates. The radius limit is taken for each fixed member to obtain the exact identity <a href="#ana:green" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ana:green">[ana:green]</a>; only then are its integrals expanded. Similarly, each finite angular type coefficient is identified with its own flux before passing to an approximant limit in the bounded functional <a href="#ana:green-functional" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ana:green-functional">[ana:green-functional]</a>. No uniform convergence of discarded derivative flux errors in either parameter or angular cutoff is used.
  - Explanation: Separates the radius limit for an actual member from the later expansion of the bounded Green integrals.
- **C10** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-cmc-jets)): Let $p\geq1$, and let $G$ be a smooth Riemannian metric jet of order $p$, with base $G_0=g_0$, whose coefficients have finite angular type. Suppose that there are angular scalar jets $C,E$, with $C_0=2m_0$ and $E_0=0$, such that $$\label{cmc:metric-asymptotics} G-b=r^{-3}\bigl(C\,\mathrm{d}\eta^2+E r^2\sigma\bigr)+o(r^{-3}).$$ For the coefficient of degree $j$, $1\leq j\leq p$, suppose that the remainder satisfies this estimate after every mixed radial and angular derivative of total order at most $p-j+1$.* *There is a smooth family of sphere jets $\Sigma_s$, $0\leq s<\infty$, given by graphs over $\{s\}\times S^2$, with the following properties:* 1. *$\Sigma_s$ has constant outward mean curvature through order $p$, and $\Sigma_0$ is minimal through that order.* 2. *The graph coefficients have finite angular type. The normal lapse $f$ for variation in $s$ has base value $f_0=1$.* 3. *If $A(s)$ and $H(s)$ are the area and mean curvature jets, then the CMC Hawking mass $$\label{cmc:hawking-definition} m_H(\Sigma_s)= \sqrt{\frac{A(s)}{16\pi}} \left(1-\frac{(H(s)^2-4)A(s)}{16\pi}\right)$$ satisfies, coefficientwise, $$\label{cmc:hawking-limit} \lim_{s\to\infty}m_H(\Sigma_s) =m_{\mathrm{AH}}(G) :=\sqrt{p_0(G)^2-\sum_{i=1}^3p_i(G)^2}.$$ Here $p_\mu(G)$ is the coefficientwise flux <a href="#eq:mass-flux" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:mass-flux">[eq:mass-flux]</a>, and the square root is its formal branch with base value $m_0>0$.
  - Explanation: Constructs CMC surface jets and identifies their limiting Hawking mass with the mass norm.
- **C11** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cmc-regularity-budget)): For the conformal jets used in the quadratic argument, the coefficient of degree one solves a homogeneous finite-mode equation $Lu=0$; Lemma <a href="#lem:inverse" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:inverse">4</a> gives all its differentiated asymptotics. The degree-two coefficient needs only the first differentiated asymptotic in Proposition <a href="#prop:cmc-jets" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:cmc-jets">10</a>, which is supplied by the given $C^{1,\alpha}_\tau$ decay of a finite-angular-type seed and the same radial equation. The fourth-order construction will be used only after the seed has been reduced to $Q(v)=\mathop{\mathrm{Hess}}v-Bv$, with $v$ a homogeneous solution containing only constant and degree-one angular modes. Then all spatially differentiated decay estimates needed above hold. These are distinct uses of the proposition; higher radial decay derivatives of an arbitrary seed are not required.
  - Explanation: Distinguishes the spatial derivative requirements of the quadratic and kernel quartic constructions.
- **C12** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-hawking-variation)): The following identity holds through the order of the sphere and metric jets: $$\label{cmc:hawking-variation} \frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}m_H =\sqrt{\frac A{16\pi}}\frac{HA}{8\pi} \left[ \bar f\left\langle \frac{\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_G+6+\|\mathrm{II}^\circ\|^2}{2} \right\rangle +\frac1{\bar f}\langle\delta f\,J\delta f\rangle \right].$$
  - Explanation: Expresses the CMC Hawking variation using the scalar defect, shear and lapse fluctuation.
- **C13** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-leading-energy)): Use the construction of Proposition <a href="#prop:cmc-jets" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:cmc-jets">10</a> through order $2l$, where $l\ge1$, and let $A_{\mathrm{leaf}}=A(0)$ be the area jet of its minimal first leaf. Suppose, as ambient and leafwise coefficient identities, respectively, that $$\label{cmc:leading-assumptions} \mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_G+6=\epsilon^{2l}\|k\|_{g_0}^2+O(\epsilon^{2l+1}), \qquad \mathrm{II}^\circ=\epsilon^lU+O(\epsilon^{l+1}), \qquad \delta f=\epsilon^lF+O(\epsilon^{l+1}).$$ Here $k$ is a smooth tensor on $M$; the fields $U,F$ on the background spheres are identified with fields on $M$. Then every coefficient below degree $2l$ of $m_{\mathrm{AH}}(G)-b_*(A_{\mathrm{leaf}})$ vanishes, and $$\label{cmc:leading-integral} \begin{split} &\bigl[m_{\mathrm{AH}}(G)-b_*(A_{\mathrm{leaf}})\bigr]_{2l}\\ &\quad=\frac1{16\pi}\int_0^\infty N(s) \int_{\{s\}\times S^2} \left(\|k\|^2+\|U\|^2+2FJ_s^0F\right)\mathrm{d}A_{g_0}\,\mathrm{d}s \ge0. \end{split}$$ All norms in this formula use the background metrics. The displayed iterated integral is finite. If it vanishes, then $k,U,F$ vanish for $s>0$, and also at the boundary by continuity.
  - Explanation: Identifies the leading coefficient with a convergent nonnegative sphere-first integral.
- **C14** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-tt-square)): Let $k$ be a smooth TT tensor of finite angular type, with decay $k=O(r^{-\gamma})$ for some $\gamma>3/2$. Let $v$ be the decaying solution of $$Lv=0, \qquad v\|_S=D^{-1}(k_{ss}\|_S). \label{eq:tt-dirichlet}$$ Then $Q(v)$ is TT, $Q(v)_{ss}\|_S=k_{ss}\|_S$, and $$\int_M N\|k\|^2\,\mathrm{d}V_0 -\kappa\int_S vDv\,\mathrm{d}A_0 =\int_M N\|k-Q(v)\|^2\,\mathrm{d}V_0. \label{eq:tt-square}$$ All integrals in this identity converge. In particular, no sign assumption on $k_{ss}\|_S$ is needed.
  - Explanation: Absorbs the boundary quadratic form into an exact weighted TT square.
- **C15** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-quadratic)): On the real linear space of all smooth TT seeds with the prescribed decay, the first deficit coefficient is zero and the second coefficient $c_2[q]$ is a continuous, rotation-invariant, nonnegative quadratic form. For a finite-angular-type seed it is given by $$\begin{gathered} c_2[q]=\frac1{16\pi}\int_M N\left( \|q-Q(v)\|^2+\|U\|^2+2FJ_s^0F\right)\,\mathrm{d}V_0,\\ J_s^0=-\Delta_{r^2\sigma}-\frac2{r^2}+\frac{6m_0}{r^3}. \end{gathered} \label{eq:quadratic-positive-form}$$ The last term is integrated on the spheres before radial integration. For every actual branch in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, $$\mathfrak D_q(\epsilon)=c_2[q]\epsilon^2+o(\epsilon^2). \label{eq:actual-quadratic-deficit}$$ In particular, $c_2[q]>0$ implies a strictly positive deficit for all sufficiently small positive $\epsilon$.
  - Explanation: Establishes the continuous nonnegative quadratic deficit form on the full seed space.
- **C16** (classification, [source](#stmt-cor-kernel)): The kernel of $c_2$ is exactly $\mathcal K$, a four-dimensional real vector space. In particular, the nonzero degree-one directions are genuine quadratic null directions.
  - Explanation: Classifies all quadratic null directions, including genuine nonradial degree-one modes.
- **C17** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-rem-kernel-sign)): If $v\|_S=c+d\cdot x$, then $$Q(v)_{ss}\|_S=D_0c+D_1d\cdot x, \qquad D_0=\frac{2\kappa}{a},\quad D_1=\frac2{a^2}+\frac{2\kappa}{a}=\frac{6m_0}{a^3}.$$ Thus $Q(v)_{ss}\|_S\geq0$ is equivalent to $D_0c\geq D_1\|d\|$. A nonzero pure dipole fails that condition, but a sufficiently large positive radial component makes a mixed radial–dipole seed satisfy it. This is a compatibility statement about seeds, not an existence claim for branches satisfying outermostness and outer area-minimization. In particular, the boundary sign cannot be used to discard the degree-one null directions from the proof.
  - Explanation: Shows why a nonnegative boundary normal component does not exclude mixed radial–dipole null directions.
- **C18** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-quartic)): Suppose that $c_2[q]=0$. Write the representation furnished by Corollary <a href="#cor:kernel" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:kernel">16</a> as $$q=Q(v):=\mathop{\mathrm{Hess}}_{g_0}v-Bv,\qquad Lv=0, \qquad v=v_0(s)+v_1(s,x),$$ where $v_1$ has only degree-one spherical modes and $v$ decays at infinity. Then $$c_0[q]=c_1[q]=c_2[q]=c_3[q]=0, \qquad c_4[q]\geq 0.$$ If $v_1\not\equiv0$, then $c_4[q]>0$. In particular, in that case the actual deficit $\mathfrak D_q(\epsilon)$ is strictly positive for all sufficiently small positive $\epsilon$.
  - Explanation: Resolves nonradial quadratic-null directions by a strictly positive quartic deficit coefficient.
- **C19** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-slice)): There is a decaying finite-angular-type function $w$, with $w\|_S=0$, for which the following construction is defined through degree four. Formally evolve the initial jets in Gaussian time and take the graph $$T=-\epsilon v+\epsilon^2w. \label{quart:time-graph}$$ The induced metric and future second fundamental form, denoted by $\widetilde g$ and $\widetilde K$, satisfy $$\begin{aligned} \widetilde K&=\epsilon^2k+O(\epsilon^3), &\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_{g_0}k&=0,&\mathop{\mathrm{div}}_{g_0}k&=0, \label{quart:leading-k}\\ \mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_{\widetilde g}+6 &=\epsilon^4\|k\|_{g_0}^2+O(\epsilon^5). \label{quart:scalar-four} \end{aligned}$$ Here $k=O(r^{-3})$, with all derivatives needed for the fourth-order CMC construction. Coefficientwise through degree four, $$\widetilde g-g_\epsilon=O(r^{-6}) \label{quart:metric-decay}$$ with the required differentiated bounds. Thus the mass covectors and their Lorentz norms agree through that degree. Moreover, $$_2 =-2vq-Bv^2-\mathrm{d}v\otimes\mathrm{d}v =-\mathop{\mathrm{Hess}}_{g_0}(v^2)+\mathrm{d}v\otimes\mathrm{d}v+Bv^2. \label{quart:metric-two}$$
  - Explanation: Removes the leading extrinsic-curvature coefficient by a corrected formal spacetime slice.
- **C20** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-null-cut)): For the changed slice of Lemma <a href="#lem:slice" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:slice">19</a> there is a sphere jet $S^\sharp$, based at $S$, such that $$\begin{aligned} \|S^\sharp\|_{\widetilde g}&=\|S\|_{g_\epsilon}+O(\epsilon^5), \label{quart:null-area}\\ H_{\widetilde g}(S^\sharp) &=\epsilon^2k_{ss}\|_S+O(\epsilon^3). \label{quart:null-h} \end{aligned}$$ Let $S_{\min}$ be the minimal Taylor leaf of the CMC construction for $\widetilde g$. Their height jets agree through degree one. If $$z=D^{-1}(k_{ss}\|_S), \label{quart:z}$$ then the height of $S^\sharp$ minus that of $S_{\min}$ is $\epsilon^2z+O(\epsilon^3)$, and $$\|S^\sharp\|_{\widetilde g}-\|S_{\min}\|_{\widetilde g} =\tfrac12\epsilon^4\int_S zDz\,\mathrm{d}A_{g_0}+O(\epsilon^5). \label{quart:area-hessian}$$ These statements remain valid when some leading boundary displacements are negative.
  - Explanation: Preserves boundary area by null transport and computes the subsequent minimal-leaf area correction.
- **C21** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-round-jet)): Let $\gamma_\epsilon=\sigma+\epsilon P_1+\epsilon^2P_2$ be a smooth metric two-jet on $S^2$ of finite angular type. Suppose its scalar curvature is angularly constant through degree two. Then there are a positive scalar two-jet $a_\epsilon$, with $a_0=1$, and a diffeomorphism two-jet $\Phi_\epsilon$, based at the identity, such that $$\gamma_\epsilon=a_\epsilon\Phi_\epsilon^*\sigma+O(\epsilon^3).$$ All coefficients can be chosen of finite angular type.
  - Explanation: Rounds a spherical metric two-jet with angularly constant curvature up to scaling and diffeomorphism.
- **C22** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-dipole-obstruction)): Let $v=v_0(s)+V(s)x$ solve $Lv=0$, where $x$ is one fixed unit-axis linear coordinate on $S^2$ and $V=O(r^{-3})$. If <a href="#quart:necessary-tangent" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="quart:necessary-tangent">[quart:necessary-tangent]</a> holds on every compact radial interval in the interior, with possibly different local vector fields and functions, then $V\equiv0$.
  - Explanation: Excludes a decaying degree-one profile under the necessary local conformal tangency condition.
- **C23** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-radial)): Suppose the fixed TT seed $q$ is invariant under rotations of $S^2$. Every branch in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> is radial for all sufficiently small $\epsilon$, and on that parameter interval $$m_{\mathrm{AH}}(\epsilon)=b_*(A_\epsilon).$$ This includes the zero seed, and requires no sign for $q_{ss}\|_S$.
  - Explanation: Uses radial uniqueness and a conserved mass identity to prove exact radial equality.

          "exact_quote": "The tensor $B$ and the static lapse satisfy $$\\begin{gathered}\n B=B_s\\,\\mathrm{d}s^2+B_t r^2\\sigma,\\qquad\n B_s=1-\\frac{2m_0}{r^3},\\qquad\n B_t=1+\\frac{m_0}{r^3},\\label{eq:B-components}\\\\\n \\nabla^2N=NB,\\qquad\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}_{g_0}B=3,\\qquad \\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_{g_0}B=0,\\qquad\n LN=0.\\label{eq:static-identities}\n\\end{gathered}$$ Moreover $D$ is positive on all spherical harmonics and $$\\label{eq:boundary-constants}\n b_*(4\\pi a^2)=m_0,\\qquad\n b_*'(4\\pi a^2)=\\frac{\\kappa}{8\\pi}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-260.8"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-lem-static"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-inverse",
        "exact_quote": "There are constants $c_\\beta,C_\\beta>0$ with the following properties. Suppose $z=o(e^{-\\beta s})$ uniformly at infinity and $$(L-V)z=F,\\qquad \\|V\\|_{C^0(M)}\\leq c_\\beta.$$ If either $z|_S=b$ or $\\partial_s z|_S=b$, then $$\\|z\\|_\\beta\\leq C_\\beta\n       \\bigl(\\|F\\|_\\beta+\\|b\\|_{C^0(S)}\\bigr).\n  \\label{ana:comparison}$$ The constants do not depend on an angular cutoff. The same estimate holds for $\\partial_s z-a_*z=b$ if $\\|a_*\\|_{C^0(S)}\\leq\\beta/2$.*\n\n\n*If $F$ and $b$ are smooth and of finite angular type, and $F=O(e^{-\\gamma s})$ for some $\\gamma>3$, there is a unique decaying finite angular type solution of $Lu=F$ with either of the above Dirichlet or Neumann data. It satisfies $$u=r^{-3}\\zeta(x)+o(r^{-3}),\\qquad\n  \\partial_s\\bigl(u-r^{-3}\\zeta(x)\\bigr)=o(r^{-3})\n  \\label{ana:inverse-asymptotic}$$ for a smooth finite angular type function $\\zeta$. These statements hold after any fixed number of angular differentiations. If $\\partial_s^jF=O(e^{-\\gamma s})$ for $0\\leq j\\leq k$, the remainder in <a href=\"#ana:inverse-asymptotic\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ana:inverse-asymptotic\">[ana:inverse-asymptotic]</a> can also be differentiated in $s$ through order $k+2$, with each resulting remainder $o(r^{-3})$.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.8"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

The same estimate holds for $\\partial_s z-a_*z=b$ if $\\|a_*\\|_{C^0(S)}\\leq\\beta/2$.*\n\n\n*If $F$ and $b$ are smooth and of finite angular type, and $F=O(e^{-\\gamma s})$ for some $\\gamma>3$, there is a unique decaying finite angular type solution of $Lu=F$ with either of the above Dirichlet or Neumann data.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ana-coefficients",
        "exact_quote": "For every smooth finite angular type seed in $C^{1,\\alpha}_\\tau$, the coefficients through degree two are well defined and $$u_j=r^{-3}\\zeta_j(x)+o(r^{-3}),\\qquad j=1,2.$$ The first coefficient has this asymptotic with arbitrarily many spatial derivatives. The second has it with the first spatial derivatives, in particular with every derivative needed at degree two below.*\n\n\n*For the fourth-order construction, suppose specifically that $$\\begin{gathered}\n q=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hess}}v-Bv,\\qquad Lv=0,\\qquad v\\longrightarrow0,\\\\\n v|_S\\text{ has only constant and degree-one spherical modes}.\n \\end{gathered}\n \\label{ana:kernel-seed}$$ Corollary <a href=\"#cor:kernel\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"cor:kernel\">16</a> will show that every seed with zero quadratic deficit has this form, so fourth-order estimates are needed only in this class. Then $q$ and all its derivatives have $O(r^{-3})$ scaled components. The coefficients $u_1,\\ldots,u_4$ exist and have the above asymptotic with arbitrarily many spatial derivatives.*\n\n\n*Let $p=2$ in the first case and $p=4$ in the second. In the coordinate $\\eta=\\operatorname{arsinh}r$, the conformal metric jet $G=(1+\\sum_{j=1}^p\\epsilon^ju_j)^4g_0$, taken through degree $p$, satisfies $$\\begin{gathered}\n G-b=r^{-3}\\bigl(C\\,\\mathrm{d}\\eta^2+E r^2\\sigma\\bigr)+o(r^{-3}),\\\\\n C=2m_0+4\\sum_{j=1}^p\\epsilon^j\\zeta_j,\\qquad\n E=4\\sum_{j=1}^p\\epsilon^j\\zeta_j.\n \\end{gathered}\n \\label{ana:metric-asymptotic}$$ For coefficient $j$, the remainder has this decay after angular and $\\eta$ derivatives through order $p-j+1$, in scaled coframes $\\mathrm{d}\\eta,r\\,\\mathrm{d}x$.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.8"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-green",
        "exact_quote": "For every sufficiently small member of the actual branch, $$p_\\mu(\\epsilon)-p_\\mu(0)\n =\\frac1{2\\pi}\\left\\{\n   \\int_S\\bigl(\\psi\\partial_sW_\\mu-W_\\mu\\partial_s\\psi\\bigr)\\,\\mathrm{d}A_{g_0}\n   -\\int_M\\bigl(W_\\mu L\\psi-\\psi LW_\\mu\\bigr)\\,\\mathrm{d}V_{g_0}\n                 \\right\\}.\n \\label{ana:green}$$ In particular the right-hand side is an absolutely convergent integral expression. More generally the linear functional $$\\mathcal G_\\mu(u,F,B)\n =\\frac1{2\\pi}\\left\\{\n \\int_S(u\\partial_sW_\\mu-W_\\mu B)\\,\\mathrm{d}A_{g_0}\n -\\int_M(W_\\mu F-uLW_\\mu)\\,\\mathrm{d}V_{g_0}\\right\\}\n \\label{ana:green-functional}$$ is bounded in the norms $\\|u\\|_\\beta+\\|F\\|_{2\\beta}+\\|B\\|_{C^0(S)}$. For finite angular type coefficient jets, this same functional computes the coefficient of their metric mass.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.8"
      },
      "evidence": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-rotation",
        "exact_quote": "Every $q\\in\\mathscr T_{\\tau,\\alpha}$ is a limit, in $\\|\\cdot\\|_\\beta$, of finite angular type tensors $q_\\nu\\in\\mathscr T_{\\tau,\\alpha}$. The approximants may be chosen as averages of rotated copies of $q$. Their weighted norms are uniformly bounded, and the construction preserves a nonnegative boundary value $q_{ss}|_S$ when that condition holds.*\n\n*The maps $q\\mapsto u_1[q]$ and $q\\mapsto u_2[q]$ defined for finite angular type seeds extend uniquely by these approximations to continuous, respectively linear and quadratic, maps on $\\mathscr T_{\\tau,\\alpha}$ with values in the weighted uniform norm. The corresponding pairs $(F_j,B_j)$ in <a href=\"#ana:first-two\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ana:first-two\">[ana:first-two]</a> extend continuously in the norms $\\|\\cdot\\|_{2\\beta}$ and $\\|\\cdot\\|_{C^0(S)}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.8"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-rotation",
          "exact_quote": "Every $q\\in\\mathscr T_{\\tau,\\alpha}$ is a limit, in $\\|\\cdot\\|_\\beta$, of finite angular type tensors $q_\\nu\\in\\mathscr T_{\\tau,\\alpha}$. The approximants may be chosen as averages of rotated copies of $q$. Their weighted norms are uniformly bounded, and the construction preserves a nonnegative boundary value $q_{ss}|_S$ when that condition holds.*\n\n*The maps $q\\mapsto u_1[q]$ and $q\\mapsto u_2[q]$ defined for finite angular type seeds extend uniquely by these approximations to continuous, respectively linear and quadratic, maps on $\\mathscr T_{\\tau,\\alpha}$ with values in the weighted uniform norm. The corresponding pairs $(F_j,B_j)$ in <a href=\"#ana:first-two\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ana:first-two\">[ana:first-two]</a> extend continuously in the norms $\\|\\cdot\\|_{2\\beta}$ and $\\|\\cdot\\|_{C^0(S)}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-260.8"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Every $q\\in\\mathscr T_{\\tau,\\alpha}$ is a limit, in $\\|\\cdot\\|_\\beta$, of finite angular type tensors $q_\\nu\\in\\mathscr T_{\\tau,\\alpha}$. The approximants may be chosen as averages of rotated copies of $q$. Their weighted norms are uniformly bounded, and the construction preserves a nonnegative boundary value $q_{ss}|_S$ when that condition holds.*\n\n*The maps $q\\mapsto u_1[q]$ and $q\\mapsto u_2[q]$ defined for finite angular type seeds extend uniquely by these approximations to continuous, respectively linear and quadratic, maps on $\\mathscr T_{\\tau,\\alpha}$ with values in the weighted uniform norm. The corresponding pairs $(F_j,B_j)$ in <a href=\"#ana:first-two\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ana:first-two\">[ana:first-two]</a> extend continuously in the norms $\\|\\cdot\\|_{2\\beta}$ and $\\|\\cdot\\|_{C^0(S)}$.",
      "explanation": "Extends coefficient functionals from finite angular type to arbitrary prescribed decaying TT seeds.",

          "exact_quote": "Every $q\\in\\mathscr T_{\\tau,\\alpha}$ is a limit, in $\\|\\cdot\\|_\\beta$, of finite angular type tensors $q_\\nu\\in\\mathscr T_{\\tau,\\alpha}$.           "exact_quote": "Every $q\\in\\mathscr T_{\\tau,\\alpha}$ is a limit, in $\\|\\cdot\\|_\\beta$, of finite angular type tensors $q_\\nu\\in\\mathscr T_{\\tau,\\alpha}$. Their weighted norms are uniformly bounded, and the construction preserves a nonnegative boundary value $q_{ss}|_S$ when that condition holds.*\n\n*The maps $q\\mapsto u_1[q]$ and $q\\mapsto u_2[q]$ defined for finite angular type seeds extend uniquely by these approximations to continuous, respectively linear and quadratic, maps on $\\mathscr T_{\\tau,\\alpha}$ with values in the weighted uniform norm. The corresponding pairs $(F_j,B_j)$ in <a href=\"#ana:first-two\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ana:first-two\">[ana:first-two]</a> extend continuously in the norms $\\|\\cdot\\|_{2\\beta}$ and $\\|\\cdot\\|_{C^0(S)}$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-expansions",
        "exact_quote": "For every prescribed seed and branch in the theorem, set $p=2$ and take $u_j,F_j,B_j$ from Lemma <a href=\"#lem:rotation\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:rotation\">7</a>. If the seed has the form <a href=\"#ana:kernel-seed\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ana:kernel-seed\">[ana:kernel-seed]</a>, we may instead take $p=4$ with the finite coefficient construction. In both cases, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\left\\|\\psi-\\sum_{j=1}^p\\epsilon^ju_j\\right\\|_\\beta\n     &=O(\\epsilon^{p+1}),\\notag\\\\\n \\left\\|L\\psi-\\sum_{j=1}^p\\epsilon^jF_j\\right\\|_{2\\beta}\n     &=O(\\epsilon^{p+1}),\\notag\\\\\n \\left\\|\\partial_s\\psi|_S-\\sum_{j=1}^p\\epsilon^jB_j\\right\\|_{C^0(S)}\n     &=O(\\epsilon^{p+1}).\n \\label{ana:three-remainders}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The area and all four mass components have actual expansions to the same order. Their mass coefficients are $$a_{\\mu j}[q]=\\mathcal G_\\mu(u_j,F_j,B_j),\\qquad\n p_\\mu(\\epsilon)=p_\\mu(0)+\\sum_{j=1}^p\\epsilon^j a_{\\mu j}[q]\n                      +O(\\epsilon^{p+1}).\n \\label{ana:mass-coefficients}$$*\n\n\n*In particular, the actual deficit satisfies $$\\mathfrak D_q(\\epsilon)=c_2[q]\\epsilon^2+O(\\epsilon^3)\n \\label{ana:deficit-second}$$ for every seed under consideration. The coefficient $c_2$ is a continuous quadratic form on $\\mathscr T_{\\tau,\\alpha}$ in the $\\|\\cdot\\|_\\beta$ topology, invariant under rotations. For seeds of the form <a href=\"#ana:kernel-seed\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ana:kernel-seed\">[ana:kernel-seed]</a> there is additionally the actual expansion $$\\mathfrak D_q(\\epsilon)\n     =c_2[q]\\epsilon^2+c_3[q]\\epsilon^3+c_4[q]\\epsilon^4+O(\\epsilon^5).\n \\label{ana:deficit-fourth}$$ The subsequent geometric arguments determine the signs and the vanishing of these coefficients.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.8"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ana-order-of-limits",
        "exact_quote": "The derivative control used in <a href=\"#ana:flux-errors\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ana:flux-errors\">[ana:flux-errors]</a> comes from each actual member’s assumed $C^{2,\\alpha}_\\tau$ decay. It is not a consequence of the weighted uniform Taylor estimates. The radius limit is taken for each fixed member to obtain the exact identity <a href=\"#ana:green\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ana:green\">[ana:green]</a>; only then are its integrals expanded. Similarly, each finite angular type coefficient is identified with its own flux before passing to an approximant limit in the bounded functional <a href=\"#ana:green-functional\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ana:green-functional\">[ana:green-functional]</a>. No uniform convergence of discarded derivative flux errors in either parameter or angular cutoff is used.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-cmc-jets",
        "exact_quote": "Let $p\\geq1$, and let $G$ be a smooth Riemannian metric jet of order $p$, with base $G_0=g_0$, whose coefficients have finite angular type. Suppose that there are angular scalar jets $C,E$, with $C_0=2m_0$ and $E_0=0$, such that $$\\label{cmc:metric-asymptotics}\n G-b=r^{-3}\\bigl(C\\,\\mathrm{d}\\eta^2+E r^2\\sigma\\bigr)+o(r^{-3}).$$ For the coefficient of degree $j$, $1\\leq j\\leq p$, suppose that the remainder satisfies this estimate after every mixed radial and angular derivative of total order at most $p-j+1$.*\n\n*There is a smooth family of sphere jets $\\Sigma_s$, $0\\leq s<\\infty$, given by graphs over $\\{s\\}\\times S^2$, with the following properties:*\n\n1.  *$\\Sigma_s$ has constant outward mean curvature through order $p$, and $\\Sigma_0$ is minimal through that order.*\n\n2.  *The graph coefficients have finite angular type. The normal lapse $f$ for variation in $s$ has base value $f_0=1$.*\n\n\n\n3.  *If $A(s)$ and $H(s)$ are the area and mean curvature jets, then the CMC Hawking mass $$\\label{cmc:hawking-definition}\n     m_H(\\Sigma_s)=\n     \\sqrt{\\frac{A(s)}{16\\pi}}\n     \\left(1-\\frac{(H(s)^2-4)A(s)}{16\\pi}\\right)$$ satisfies, coefficientwise, $$\\label{cmc:hawking-limit}\n     \\lim_{s\\to\\infty}m_H(\\Sigma_s)\n     =m_{\\mathrm{AH}}(G)\n     :=\\sqrt{p_0(G)^2-\\sum_{i=1}^3p_i(G)^2}.$$ Here $p_\\mu(G)$ is the coefficientwise flux <a href=\"#eq:mass-flux\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:mass-flux\">[eq:mass-flux]</a>, and the square root is its formal branch with base value $m_0>0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.8"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-cmc-jets",
          "exact_quote": "Let $p\\geq1$, and let $G$ be a smooth Riemannian metric jet of order $p$, with base $G_0=g_0$, whose coefficients have finite angular type. Suppose that there are angular scalar jets $C,E$, with $C_0=2m_0$ and $E_0=0$, such that $$\\label{cmc:metric-asymptotics}\n G-b=r^{-3}\\bigl(C\\,\\mathrm{d}\\eta^2+E r^2\\sigma\\bigr)+o(r^{-3}).$$ For the coefficient of degree $j$, $1\\leq j\\leq p$, suppose that the remainder satisfies this estimate after every mixed radial and angular derivative of total order at most $p-j+1$.*\n\n*There is a smooth family of sphere jets $\\Sigma_s$, $0\\leq s<\\infty$, given by graphs over $\\{s\\}\\times S^2$, with the following properties:*\n\n1.  *$\\Sigma_s$ has constant outward mean curvature through order $p$, and $\\Sigma_0$ is minimal through that order.*\n\n2.  *The graph coefficients have finite angular type. The normal lapse $f$ for variation in $s$ has base value $f_0=1$.*\n\n\n\n3.  *If $A(s)$ and $H(s)$ are the area and mean curvature jets, then the CMC Hawking mass $$\\label{cmc:hawking-definition}\n     m_H(\\Sigma_s)=\n     \\sqrt{\\frac{A(s)}{16\\pi}}\n     \\left(1-\\frac{(H(s)^2-4)A(s)}{16\\pi}\\right)$$ satisfies, coefficientwise, $$\\label{cmc:hawking-limit}\n     \\lim_{s\\to\\infty}m_H(\\Sigma_s)\n     =m_{\\mathrm{AH}}(G)\n     :=\\sqrt{p_0(G)^2-\\sum_{i=1}^3p_i(G)^2}.$$ Here $p_\\mu(G)$ is the coefficientwise flux <a href=\"#eq:mass-flux\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:mass-flux\">[eq:mass-flux]</a>, and the square root is its formal branch with base value $m_0>0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-260.8"
        }

The normal lapse $f$ for variation in $s$ has base value $f_0=1$.*\n\n\n\n3.  *If $A(s)$ and $H(s)$ are the area and mean curvature jets, then the CMC Hawking mass $$\\label{cmc:hawking-definition}\n     m_H(\\Sigma_s)=\n     \\sqrt{\\frac{A(s)}{16\\pi}}\n     \\left(1-\\frac{(H(s)^2-4)A(s)}{16\\pi}\\right)$$ satisfies, coefficientwise, $$\\label{cmc:hawking-limit}\n     \\lim_{s\\to\\infty}m_H(\\Sigma_s)\n     =m_{\\mathrm{AH}}(G)\n     :=\\sqrt{p_0(G)^2-\\sum_{i=1}^3p_i(G)^2}.$$ Here $p_\\mu(G)$ is the coefficientwise flux <a href=\"#eq:mass-flux\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:mass-flux\">[eq:mass-flux]</a>, and the square root is its formal branch with base value $m_0>0$.",

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-cmc-jets",
          "exact_quote": "Let $p\\geq1$, and let $G$ be a smooth Riemannian metric jet of order $p$, with base $G_0=g_0$, whose coefficients have finite angular type. Suppose that there are angular scalar jets $C,E$, with $C_0=2m_0$ and $E_0=0$, such that $$\\label{cmc:metric-asymptotics}\n G-b=r^{-3}\\bigl(C\\,\\mathrm{d}\\eta^2+E r^2\\sigma\\bigr)+o(r^{-3}).$$ For the coefficient of degree $j$, $1\\leq j\\leq p$, suppose that the remainder satisfies this estimate after every mixed radial and angular derivative of total order at most $p-j+1$.*\n\n*There is a smooth family of sphere jets $\\Sigma_s$, $0\\leq s<\\infty$, given by graphs over $\\{s\\}\\times S^2$, with the following properties:*\n\n1.  *$\\Sigma_s$ has constant outward mean curvature through order $p$, and $\\Sigma_0$ is minimal through that order.*\n\n2.  *The graph coefficients have finite angular type. The normal lapse $f$ for variation in $s$ has base value $f_0=1$.*\n\n\n\n3.  *If $A(s)$ and $H(s)$ are the area and mean curvature jets, then the CMC Hawking mass $$\\label{cmc:hawking-definition}\n     m_H(\\Sigma_s)=\n     \\sqrt{\\frac{A(s)}{16\\pi}}\n     \\left(1-\\frac{(H(s)^2-4)A(s)}{16\\pi}\\right)$$ satisfies, coefficientwise, $$\\label{cmc:hawking-limit}\n     \\lim_{s\\to\\infty}m_H(\\Sigma_s)\n     =m_{\\mathrm{AH}}(G)\n     :=\\sqrt{p_0(G)^2-\\sum_{i=1}^3p_i(G)^2}.$$ Here $p_\\mu(G)$ is the coefficientwise flux <a href=\"#eq:mass-flux\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:mass-flux\">[eq:mass-flux]</a>, and the square root is its formal branch with base value $m_0>0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-260.8"
        }
      ],
      "id": "C10",
      "kind": "construction",
      "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-cmc-jets",
          "exact_quote": "Let $p\\geq1$, and let $G$ be a smooth Riemannian metric jet of order $p$, with base $G_0=g_0$, whose coefficients have finite angular type. Suppose that there are angular scalar jets $C,E$, with $C_0=2m_0$ and $E_0=0$, such that $$\\label{cmc:metric-asymptotics}\n G-b=r^{-3}\\bigl(C\\,\\mathrm{d}\\eta^2+E r^2\\sigma\\bigr)+o(r^{-3}).$$ For the coefficient of degree $j$, $1\\leq j\\leq p$, suppose that the remainder satisfies this estimate after every mixed radial and angular derivative of total order at most $p-j+1$.*\n\n*There is a smooth family of sphere jets $\\Sigma_s$, $0\\leq s<\\infty$, given by graphs over $\\{s\\}\\times S^2$, with the following properties:*\n\n1.  *$\\Sigma_s$ has constant outward mean curvature through order $p$, and $\\Sigma_0$ is minimal through that order.*\n\n2.  *The graph coefficients have finite angular type. The normal lapse $f$ for variation in $s$ has base value $f_0=1$.*\n\n\n\n3.  *If $A(s)$ and $H(s)$ are the area and mean curvature jets, then the CMC Hawking mass $$\\label{cmc:hawking-definition}\n     m_H(\\Sigma_s)=\n     \\sqrt{\\frac{A(s)}{16\\pi}}\n     \\left(1-\\frac{(H(s)^2-4)A(s)}{16\\pi}\\right)$$ satisfies, coefficientwise, $$\\label{cmc:hawking-limit}\n     \\lim_{s\\to\\infty}m_H(\\Sigma_s)\n     =m_{\\mathrm{AH}}(G)\n     :=\\sqrt{p_0(G)^2-\\sum_{i=1}^3p_i(G)^2}.$$ Here $p_\\mu(G)$ is the coefficientwise flux <a href=\"#eq:mass-flux\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:mass-flux\">[eq:mass-flux]</a>, and the square root is its formal branch with base value $m_0>0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-260.8"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cmc-regularity-budget",
        "exact_quote": "For the conformal jets used in the quadratic argument, the coefficient of degree one solves a homogeneous finite-mode equation $Lu=0$; Lemma <a href=\"#lem:inverse\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:inverse\">4</a> gives all its differentiated asymptotics. The degree-two coefficient needs only the first differentiated asymptotic in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:cmc-jets\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:cmc-jets\">10</a>, which is supplied by the given $C^{1,\\alpha}_\\tau$ decay of a finite-angular-type seed and the same radial equation. The fourth-order construction will be used only after the seed has been reduced to $Q(v)=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hess}}v-Bv$, with $v$ a homogeneous solution containing only constant and degree-one angular modes. Then all spatially differentiated decay estimates needed above hold. These are distinct uses of the proposition; higher radial decay derivatives of an arbitrary seed are not required.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.8"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-hawking-variation",
        "exact_quote": "The following identity holds through the order of the sphere and metric jets: $$\\label{cmc:hawking-variation}\n \\frac{\\mathrm{d}}{\\mathrm{d}s}m_H\n =\\sqrt{\\frac A{16\\pi}}\\frac{HA}{8\\pi}\n \\left[\n \\bar f\\left\\langle\n      \\frac{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Scal}}_G+6+|\\mathrm{II}^\\circ|^2}{2}\n       \\right\\rangle\n +\\frac1{\\bar f}\\langle\\delta f\\,J\\delta f\\rangle\n \\right].$$",
        "source_id": "PM-260.8"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-hawking-variation",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-leading-energy",
        "exact_quote": "Use the construction of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:cmc-jets\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:cmc-jets\">10</a> through order $2l$, where $l\\ge1$, and let $A_{\\mathrm{leaf}}=A(0)$ be the area jet of its minimal first leaf. Suppose, as ambient and leafwise coefficient identities, respectively, that $$\\label{cmc:leading-assumptions}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Scal}}_G+6=\\epsilon^{2l}|k|_{g_0}^2+O(\\epsilon^{2l+1}),\n \\qquad\n \\mathrm{II}^\\circ=\\epsilon^lU+O(\\epsilon^{l+1}),\n \\qquad\n \\delta f=\\epsilon^lF+O(\\epsilon^{l+1}).$$ Here $k$ is a smooth tensor on $M$; the fields $U,F$ on the background spheres are identified with fields on $M$. Then every coefficient below degree $2l$ of $m_{\\mathrm{AH}}(G)-b_*(A_{\\mathrm{leaf}})$ vanishes, and $$\\label{cmc:leading-integral}\n \\begin{split}\n &\\bigl[m_{\\mathrm{AH}}(G)-b_*(A_{\\mathrm{leaf}})\\bigr]_{2l}\\\\\n &\\quad=\\frac1{16\\pi}\\int_0^\\infty N(s)\n   \\int_{\\{s\\}\\times S^2}\n    \\left(|k|^2+|U|^2+2FJ_s^0F\\right)\\mathrm{d}A_{g_0}\\,\\mathrm{d}s\n \\ge0.\n \\end{split}$$ All norms in this formula use the background metrics. The displayed iterated integral is finite. If it vanishes, then $k,U,F$ vanish for $s>0$, and also at the boundary by continuity.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.8"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-tt-square",
        "exact_quote": "Let $k$ be a smooth TT tensor of finite angular type, with decay $k=O(r^{-\\gamma})$ for some $\\gamma>3/2$. Let $v$ be the decaying solution of $$Lv=0,\n \\qquad v|_S=D^{-1}(k_{ss}|_S).\n \\label{eq:tt-dirichlet}$$ Then $Q(v)$ is TT, $Q(v)_{ss}|_S=k_{ss}|_S$, and $$\\int_M N|k|^2\\,\\mathrm{d}V_0\n -\\kappa\\int_S vDv\\,\\mathrm{d}A_0\n =\\int_M N|k-Q(v)|^2\\,\\mathrm{d}V_0.\n \\label{eq:tt-square}$$ All integrals in this identity converge. In particular, no sign assumption on $k_{ss}|_S$ is needed.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.8"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-tt-square",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-quadratic",
        "exact_quote": "On the real linear space of all smooth TT seeds with the prescribed decay, the first deficit coefficient is zero and the second coefficient $c_2[q]$ is a continuous, rotation-invariant, nonnegative quadratic form. For a finite-angular-type seed it is given by $$\\begin{gathered}\n c_2[q]=\\frac1{16\\pi}\\int_M N\\left(\n |q-Q(v)|^2+|U|^2+2FJ_s^0F\\right)\\,\\mathrm{d}V_0,\\\\\n J_s^0=-\\Delta_{r^2\\sigma}-\\frac2{r^2}+\\frac{6m_0}{r^3}.\n \\end{gathered}\n \\label{eq:quadratic-positive-form}$$ The last term is integrated on the spheres before radial integration. For every actual branch in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a>, $$\\mathfrak D_q(\\epsilon)=c_2[q]\\epsilon^2+o(\\epsilon^2).\n \\label{eq:actual-quadratic-deficit}$$ In particular, $c_2[q]>0$ implies a strictly positive deficit for all sufficiently small positive $\\epsilon$.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.8"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-quadratic",
          "exact_quote": "On the real linear space of all smooth TT seeds with the prescribed decay, the first deficit coefficient is zero and the second coefficient $c_2[q]$ is a continuous, rotation-invariant, nonnegative quadratic form. For a finite-angular-type seed it is given by $$\\begin{gathered}\n c_2[q]=\\frac1{16\\pi}\\int_M N\\left(\n |q-Q(v)|^2+|U|^2+2FJ_s^0F\\right)\\,\\mathrm{d}V_0,\\\\\n J_s^0=-\\Delta_{r^2\\sigma}-\\frac2{r^2}+\\frac{6m_0}{r^3}.\n \\end{gathered}\n \\label{eq:quadratic-positive-form}$$ The last term is integrated on the spheres before radial integration. For every actual branch in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a>, $$\\mathfrak D_q(\\epsilon)=c_2[q]\\epsilon^2+o(\\epsilon^2).\n \\label{eq:actual-quadratic-deficit}$$ In particular, $c_2[q]>0$ implies a strictly positive deficit for all sufficiently small positive $\\epsilon$.",
          "source_id": "PM-260.8"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "On the real linear space of all smooth TT seeds with the prescribed decay, the first deficit coefficient is zero and the second coefficient $c_2[q]$ is a continuous, rotation-invariant, nonnegative quadratic form. For every actual branch in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a>, $$\\mathfrak D_q(\\epsilon)=c_2[q]\\epsilon^2+o(\\epsilon^2).\n \\label{eq:actual-quadratic-deficit}$$ In particular, $c_2[q]>0$ implies a strictly positive deficit for all sufficiently small positive $\\epsilon$.",

For a finite-angular-type seed it is given by $$\\begin{gathered}\n c_2[q]=\\frac1{16\\pi}\\int_M N\\left(\n |q-Q(v)|^2+|U|^2+2FJ_s^0F\\right)\\,\\mathrm{d}V_0,\\\\\n J_s^0=-\\Delta_{r^2\\sigma}-\\frac2{r^2}+\\frac{6m_0}{r^3}.\n \\end{gathered}\n \\label{eq:quadratic-positive-form}$$ The last term is integrated on the spheres before radial integration. For every actual branch in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a>, $$\\mathfrak D_q(\\epsilon)=c_2[q]\\epsilon^2+o(\\epsilon^2).\n \\label{eq:actual-quadratic-deficit}$$ In particular, $c_2[q]>0$ implies a strictly positive deficit for all sufficiently small positive $\\epsilon$.",

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-quadratic",
          "exact_quote": "On the real linear space of all smooth TT seeds with the prescribed decay, the first deficit coefficient is zero and the second coefficient $c_2[q]$ is a continuous, rotation-invariant, nonnegative quadratic form. For a finite-angular-type seed it is given by $$\\begin{gathered}\n c_2[q]=\\frac1{16\\pi}\\int_M N\\left(\n |q-Q(v)|^2+|U|^2+2FJ_s^0F\\right)\\,\\mathrm{d}V_0,\\\\\n J_s^0=-\\Delta_{r^2\\sigma}-\\frac2{r^2}+\\frac{6m_0}{r^3}.\n \\end{gathered}\n \\label{eq:quadratic-positive-form}$$ The last term is integrated on the spheres before radial integration. For every actual branch in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a>, $$\\mathfrak D_q(\\epsilon)=c_2[q]\\epsilon^2+o(\\epsilon^2).\n \\label{eq:actual-quadratic-deficit}$$ In particular, $c_2[q]>0$ implies a strictly positive deficit for all sufficiently small positive $\\epsilon$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rem-kernel-sign",
        "exact_quote": "If $v|_S=c+d\\cdot x$, then $$Q(v)_{ss}|_S=D_0c+D_1d\\cdot x,\n \\qquad D_0=\\frac{2\\kappa}{a},\\quad\n D_1=\\frac2{a^2}+\\frac{2\\kappa}{a}=\\frac{6m_0}{a^3}.$$ Thus $Q(v)_{ss}|_S\\geq0$ is equivalent to $D_0c\\geq D_1|d|$. A nonzero pure dipole fails that condition, but a sufficiently large positive radial component makes a mixed radial–dipole seed satisfy it. This is a compatibility statement about seeds, not an existence claim for branches satisfying outermostness and outer area-minimization. In particular, the boundary sign cannot be used to discard the degree-one null directions from the proof.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.8"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

This is a compatibility statement about seeds, not an existence claim for branches satisfying outermostness and outer area-minimization.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-quartic",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that $c_2[q]=0$. Write the representation furnished by Corollary <a href=\"#cor:kernel\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"cor:kernel\">16</a> as $$q=Q(v):=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hess}}_{g_0}v-Bv,\\qquad Lv=0,\n \\qquad v=v_0(s)+v_1(s,x),$$ where $v_1$ has only degree-one spherical modes and $v$ decays at infinity. Then $$c_0[q]=c_1[q]=c_2[q]=c_3[q]=0,\n \\qquad c_4[q]\\geq 0.$$ If $v_1\\not\\equiv0$, then $c_4[q]>0$. In particular, in that case the actual deficit $\\mathfrak D_q(\\epsilon)$ is strictly positive for all sufficiently small positive $\\epsilon$.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.8"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-quartic",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose that $c_2[q]=0$. Then $$c_0[q]=c_1[q]=c_2[q]=c_3[q]=0,\n \\qquad c_4[q]\\geq 0.$$ If $v_1\\not\\equiv0$, then $c_4[q]>0$.           "source_id": "PM-260.8"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Suppose that $c_2[q]=0$.       "id": "C18",
      "kind": "theorem",
      "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-quartic",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose that $c_2[q]=0$. Write the representation furnished by Corollary <a href=\"#cor:kernel\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"cor:kernel\">16</a> as $$q=Q(v):=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hess}}_{g_0}v-Bv,\\qquad Lv=0,\n \\qquad v=v_0(s)+v_1(s,x),$$ where $v_1$ has only degree-one spherical modes and $v$ decays at infinity.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-slice",
        "exact_quote": "There is a decaying finite-angular-type function $w$, with $w|_S=0$, for which the following construction is defined through degree four. Formally evolve the initial jets in Gaussian time and take the graph $$T=-\\epsilon v+\\epsilon^2w.\n \\label{quart:time-graph}$$ The induced metric and future second fundamental form, denoted by $\\widetilde g$ and $\\widetilde K$, satisfy $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\widetilde K&=\\epsilon^2k+O(\\epsilon^3),\n &\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}_{g_0}k&=0,&\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_{g_0}k&=0,\n \\label{quart:leading-k}\\\\\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Scal}}_{\\widetilde g}+6\n   &=\\epsilon^4|k|_{g_0}^2+O(\\epsilon^5).\n \\label{quart:scalar-four}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $k=O(r^{-3})$, with all derivatives needed for the fourth-order CMC construction. Coefficientwise through degree four, $$\\widetilde g-g_\\epsilon=O(r^{-6})\n \\label{quart:metric-decay}$$ with the required differentiated bounds. Thus the mass covectors and their Lorentz norms agree through that degree. Moreover, $$_2\n   =-2vq-Bv^2-\\mathrm{d}v\\otimes\\mathrm{d}v\n   =-\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hess}}_{g_0}(v^2)+\\mathrm{d}v\\otimes\\mathrm{d}v+Bv^2.\n \\label{quart:metric-two}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-260.8"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-slice",
          "exact_quote": "There is a decaying finite-angular-type function $w$, with $w|_S=0$, for which the following construction is defined through degree four. Coefficientwise through degree four, $$\\widetilde g-g_\\epsilon=O(r^{-6})\n \\label{quart:metric-decay}$$ with the required differentiated bounds. Thus the mass covectors and their Lorentz norms agree through that degree. Moreover, $$_2\n   =-2vq-Bv^2-\\mathrm{d}v\\otimes\\mathrm{d}v\n   =-\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hess}}_{g_0}(v^2)+\\mathrm{d}v\\otimes\\mathrm{d}v+Bv^2.\n \\label{quart:metric-two}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-260.8"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-null-cut",
        "exact_quote": "For the changed slice of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:slice\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:slice\">19</a> there is a sphere jet $S^\\sharp$, based at $S$, such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n |S^\\sharp|_{\\widetilde g}&=|S|_{g_\\epsilon}+O(\\epsilon^5),\n \\label{quart:null-area}\\\\\n H_{\\widetilde g}(S^\\sharp)\n      &=\\epsilon^2k_{ss}|_S+O(\\epsilon^3).\n \\label{quart:null-h}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Let $S_{\\min}$ be the minimal Taylor leaf of the CMC construction for $\\widetilde g$. Their height jets agree through degree one. If $$z=D^{-1}(k_{ss}|_S),\n \\label{quart:z}$$ then the height of $S^\\sharp$ minus that of $S_{\\min}$ is $\\epsilon^2z+O(\\epsilon^3)$, and $$|S^\\sharp|_{\\widetilde g}-|S_{\\min}|_{\\widetilde g}\n     =\\tfrac12\\epsilon^4\\int_S zDz\\,\\mathrm{d}A_{g_0}+O(\\epsilon^5).\n \\label{quart:area-hessian}$$ These statements remain valid when some leading boundary displacements are negative.",
        "source_id": "PM-260.8"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-null-cut",
          "exact_quote": "For the changed slice of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:slice\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:slice\">19</a> there is a sphere jet $S^\\sharp$, based at $S$, such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n |S^\\sharp|_{\\widetilde g}&=|S|_{g_\\epsilon}+O(\\epsilon^5),\n \\label{quart:null-area}\\\\\n H_{\\widetilde g}(S^\\sharp)\n      &=\\epsilon^2k_{ss}|_S+O(\\epsilon^3).\n \\label{quart:null-h}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Let $S_{\\min}$ be the minimal Taylor leaf of the CMC construction for $\\widetilde g$. Their height jets agree through degree one. If $$z=D^{-1}(k_{ss}|_S),\n \\label{quart:z}$$ then the height of $S^\\sharp$ minus that of $S_{\\min}$ is $\\epsilon^2z+O(\\epsilon^3)$, and $$|S^\\sharp|_{\\widetilde g}-|S_{\\min}|_{\\widetilde g}\n     =\\tfrac12\\epsilon^4\\int_S zDz\\,\\mathrm{d}A_{g_0}+O(\\epsilon^5).\n \\label{quart:area-hessian}$$ These statements remain valid when some leading boundary displacements are negative.",
          "source_id": "PM-260.8"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For the changed slice of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:slice\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:slice\">19</a> there is a sphere jet $S^\\sharp$, based at $S$, such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n |S^\\sharp|_{\\widetilde g}&=|S|_{g_\\epsilon}+O(\\epsilon^5),\n \\label{quart:null-area}\\\\\n H_{\\widetilde g}(S^\\sharp)\n      &=\\epsilon^2k_{ss}|_S+O(\\epsilon^3).\n \\label{quart:null-h}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Let $S_{\\min}$ be the minimal Taylor leaf of the CMC construction for $\\widetilde g$. If $$z=D^{-1}(k_{ss}|_S),\n \\label{quart:z}$$ then the height of $S^\\sharp$ minus that of $S_{\\min}$ is $\\epsilon^2z+O(\\epsilon^3)$, and $$|S^\\sharp|_{\\widetilde g}-|S_{\\min}|_{\\widetilde g}\n     =\\tfrac12\\epsilon^4\\int_S zDz\\,\\mathrm{d}A_{g_0}+O(\\epsilon^5).\n \\label{quart:area-hessian}$$ These statements remain valid when some leading boundary displacements are negative.",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-null-cut",
          "exact_quote": "For the changed slice of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:slice\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:slice\">19</a> there is a sphere jet $S^\\sharp$, based at $S$, such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n |S^\\sharp|_{\\widetilde g}&=|S|_{g_\\epsilon}+O(\\epsilon^5),\n \\label{quart:null-area}\\\\\n H_{\\widetilde g}(S^\\sharp)\n      &=\\epsilon^2k_{ss}|_S+O(\\epsilon^3).\n \\label{quart:null-h}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Let $S_{\\min}$ be the minimal Taylor leaf of the CMC construction for $\\widetilde g$. Their height jets agree through degree one. If $$z=D^{-1}(k_{ss}|_S),\n \\label{quart:z}$$ then the height of $S^\\sharp$ minus that of $S_{\\min}$ is $\\epsilon^2z+O(\\epsilon^3)$, and $$|S^\\sharp|_{\\widetilde g}-|S_{\\min}|_{\\widetilde g}\n     =\\tfrac12\\epsilon^4\\int_S zDz\\,\\mathrm{d}A_{g_0}+O(\\epsilon^5).\n \\label{quart:area-hessian}$$ These statements remain valid when some leading boundary displacements are negative.",
          "source_id": "PM-260.8"
        }

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Radial seed** ([source](#pm-h-6)): A tensor is called *radial* if it is invariant under every rotation of the $S^2$ factor.
- **Future MOTS** ([source](#pm-h-5)): A future marginally outer trapped surface, or MOTS, has $\theta_+=0$.
- **Finite angular type** ([source](#sec:taylor)): Finite angular type means that the span of all rotational pullbacks of the function or tensor is finite-dimensional.
- **Weighted function norm** ([source](#sec:taylor)): For a tensor $T$ we also use the weighted function norm $$\\|T\\|_\beta=\sup_M e^{\beta s}\|T\|_{g_0}.$$
- **Penrose comparison and deficit** ([source](#pm-h-6)): Define $$\label{eq:deficit} b_*(A)=\sqrt{\frac A{16\pi}}\left(1+\frac A{4\pi}\right), \qquad \mathfrak D_q(\epsilon)=m_{\mathrm{AH}}(\epsilon)-b_*(A_\epsilon).$$
- **Quadratic kernel space** ([source](#pm-h-26)): Define the fixed vector space $$\mathcal K=\left\{Q(v):Lv=0,\ v\text{ decays},\quad v\|_S\in\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb R}\{1,x_1,x_2,x_3\}\right\}. \label{eq:kernel-space}$$
- **Hyperbolic mass norm** ([source](#pm-h-5)): Thus $$m_{\mathrm{AH}}(\epsilon)=\sqrt{p_0(\epsilon)^2-\sum_{i=1}^3p_i(\epsilon)^2}$$ is the positive mass norm.
- **Actual conformal data** ([source](#eq:branch)): Set $$\label{eq:actual-data} g_\epsilon=\phi_\epsilon^4g_0,\qquad K_\epsilon=\epsilon\phi_\epsilon^{-2}q,\qquad A_\epsilon=\|S\|_{g_\epsilon}.$$
- **Static TT potential operator** ([source](#sec:quadratic)): Introduce $$Q(v)=\mathop{\mathrm{Hess}}_{g_0}v-vB.$$

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-lem-inverse) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-inverse): Weighted comparison and separated radial equations -> Scalar inverse and asymptotics
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-lem-green) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-green): Finite-radius Green identity followed by the radius limit -> Bounded mass functional
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-lem-rotation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rotation): Positive rotation averaging and coefficient continuity -> Full-space coefficient maps
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-prop-expansions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-expansions): Nonlinear remainder comparison and bounded Green evaluation -> Actual deficit expansions
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-prop-cmc-jets) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-cmc-jets): Jacobi inversion, boosted asymptotic graphs and finite Taylor recursion -> CMC sphere jets with mass limit
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-prop-leading-energy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-leading-energy): Integrate the leading Hawking variation at finite radius before taking the endpoint limit -> Nonnegative leading coefficient
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-lem-tt-square) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-tt-square): Static integration by parts and completion of the weighted square -> Boundary-corrected TT square
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-prop-quadratic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-quadratic): Minimal-leaf area Hessian and TT square absorption -> Nonnegative quadratic deficit
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-cor-kernel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-kernel): Equality conditions, spherical Hessian and closed rotation-invariant null space -> Complete quadratic kernel
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-prop-quartic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-dipole-obstruction-2): Corrected slice, null transport, quartic square and dipole obstruction -> Strict deficit in nonradial null directions
- **R11** [claim](#stmt-prop-radial) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-radial): Rotation comparison and radial conserved mass -> Exact radial equality
- **R12** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-radial-2): Separate radial, quadratic-positive and nonradial quadratic-null seeds -> Local nonlinear inequality and rigidity

It satisfies $$u=r^{-3}\\zeta(x)+o(r^{-3}),\\qquad\n  \\partial_s\\bigl(u-r^{-3}\\zeta(x)\\bigr)=o(r^{-3})\n  \\label{ana:inverse-asymptotic}$$ for a smooth finite angular type function $\\zeta$. These statements hold after any fixed number of angular differentiations. If $\\partial_s^jF=O(e^{-\\gamma s})$ for $0\\leq j\\leq k$, the remainder in <a href=\"#ana:inverse-asymptotic\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ana:inverse-asymptotic\">[ana:inverse-asymptotic]</a> can also be differentiated in $s$ through order $k+2$, with each resulting remainder $o(r^{-3})$.",

The coefficient $c_2$ is a continuous quadratic form on $\\mathscr T_{\\tau,\\alpha}$ in the $\\|\\cdot\\|_\\beta$ topology, invariant under rotations. The normal lapse $f$ for variation in $s$ has base value $f_0=1$.*\n\n\n\n3.  *If $A(s)$ and $H(s)$ are the area and mean curvature jets, then the CMC Hawking mass $$\\label{cmc:hawking-definition}\n     m_H(\\Sigma_s)=\n     \\sqrt{\\frac{A(s)}{16\\pi}}\n     \\left(1-\\frac{(H(s)^2-4)A(s)}{16\\pi}\\right)$$ satisfies, coefficientwise, $$\\label{cmc:hawking-limit}\n     \\lim_{s\\to\\infty}m_H(\\Sigma_s)\n     =m_{\\mathrm{AH}}(G)\n     :=\\sqrt{p_0(G)^2-\\sum_{i=1}^3p_i(G)^2}.$$ Here $p_\\mu(G)$ is the coefficientwise flux <a href=\"#eq:mass-flux\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:mass-flux\">[eq:mass-flux]</a>, and the square root is its formal branch with base value $m_0>0$.",

          "exact_quote": "For every background, fixed TT seed, and solution branch satisfying <a href=\"#eq:background\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:background\">[eq:background]</a>–<a href=\"#eq:branch\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:branch\">[eq:branch]</a> and the mass assumptions above, there is $0<\\epsilon_0\\leq\\epsilon_*$ such that $$m_{\\mathrm{AH}}(\\epsilon)\\geq\n \\sqrt{\\frac{A_\\epsilon}{16\\pi}}\\left(1+\\frac{A_\\epsilon}{4\\pi}\\right)\n \\qquad(0\\leq\\epsilon<\\epsilon_0).$$ For all sufficiently small positive $\\epsilon$, equality holds if the seed is radial, and the inequality is strict otherwise.",
          "source_id": "PM-260.8"
        }
      ],

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **local_mass_area**: The hyperbolic mass norm bounds the comparison function of the marginally outer trapped boundary area on the stated local interval.
- **radial_rigidity**: Radial seeds give exact equality; nonradial seeds give strict inequality at sufficiently small positive parameters.
- **quadratic_null**: A vanishing quadratic deficit does not imply radiality; degree-one null directions require the quartic argument.
- **radial_mass**: The radial conserved mass is constant, including at turning points of the area radius.
- **limit_order**: Obtain the exact mass identity at fixed parameter before expanding its convergent integrals.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **large_perturbation**: Apply the bound at an arbitrary positive perturbation parameter. -> The local conclusion supplies no admissible interval for such a parameter.
- **branch_existence**: Treat the theorem as a solver that produces a branch for any transverse-traceless seed. -> Conditional branch analysis cannot certify existence of the requested branch.
- **quadratic_only**: Infer equality or radiality from a zero quadratic deficit. -> Nonzero degree-one directions are quadratic null directions.
- **actual_foliation**: Use the formal surface or slice jets as actual geometric objects at a positive parameter. -> The retained Taylor identities do not provide such existence.
- **mass_interchange**: Infer derivative flux control from the weighted uniform Taylor remainder and interchange the limits. -> The mass identification uses actual-member derivative decay before expansion.
- **wrong_charges**: Interpret the spatial hyperbolic mass components as angular momentum. -> The Lorentz mass norm would be formed from the wrong charges.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The result is conditional on a prescribed decaying solution branch on an unbounded exterior and a sufficiently small interval depending on the fixed seed and branch. No branch existence for every seed is asserted. The surface and slice constructions are finite Taylor identities, not actual geometric objects at a nonzero parameter; evaluating their algebraic expressions does not decide the exterior inequality or its hypotheses.

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **LIMIT-01** (hypothesis): The result is conditional on a fixed seed and a given solution branch; it does not establish branch existence for every seed. Blocked use: ['Use as a general existence theorem or as a solver guaranteed to construct admissible branches.']
- **LIMIT-02** (hypothesis): The transverse-traceless seed is independent of the perturbation parameter. Blocked use: ['Apply the fixed-seed conclusion to a parameter-dependent family of seeds without an additional argument.']
- **LIMIT-03** (nonconstructive): The admissible parameter interval depends on the fixed seed and branch; a uniform positive deficit coefficient bound is not asserted. Blocked use: ['Claim a uniform interval across seeds or branches, or supply a numerical smallness threshold from this theorem.']
- **LIMIT-04** (hypothesis): The seed satisfies the stated smoothness and weighted decay hypotheses. Blocked use: ['Use the theorem for seeds outside the prescribed weighted space or without the transverse-traceless constraints.']
- **LIMIT-05** (hypothesis): The mass-flux limits must be finite and the mass covector future timelike for sufficiently small positive parameters. Blocked use: ['Replace the Lorentz mass norm by an arbitrary charge or apply the theorem when the mass assumptions are unproved.']
- **LIMIT-06** (edge_case): Spatial components of the hyperbolic mass covector are not angular momentum charges. Blocked use: ['Interpret the spatial mass components as angular momentum or substitute a rotating black-hole inequality.']
- **LIMIT-07** (edge_case): The theorem uses the boundary area without a sign restriction on its normal seed component, outermostness, or outer area-minimization. Blocked use: ['Reject the main theorem solely because those extra boundary conditions are absent.']
- **LIMIT-08** (hypothesis): The enclosing-area corollary adds an outermostness condition that excludes weakly future outer trapped enclosing surfaces, not merely additional marginally outer trapped surfaces. Blocked use: ['Infer the enclosing-area conclusion by checking only for additional marginally outer trapped surfaces.']
- **LIMIT-09** (formal_scope): The CMC construction uses finite Taylor surface families, without requiring an actual global foliation at a nonzero parameter. Blocked use: ['Claim an actual global CMC foliation from the jet construction alone.']
- **LIMIT-10** (formal_scope): The fourth-order geometric objects are jet identities and do not assert existence at a nonzero parameter. Blocked use: ['Interpret the formal changed slice or transported boundary as an actually constructed spacetime object.']
- **LIMIT-11** (approximation): Mass identification takes the radius limit for a fixed branch member before expansion; discarded derivative flux errors need not converge uniformly in the parameter or angular cutoff. Blocked use: ['Exchange the radius, parameter, or approximation limits without establishing the additional uniform control.']
- **LIMIT-12** (edge_case): Nonradial degree-one directions remain quadratic-null; a nonnegative boundary normal component does not remove them from the proof. Blocked use: ['Conclude radiality or exact equality from a vanishing quadratic deficit, or discard mixed radial–dipole null directions using the boundary sign.']
- **LIMIT-13** (formal_scope): The theorem does not supply a general spacetime Penrose inequality, a constrained extension across the boundary, or a global spacetime development. Blocked use: ['Transfer the conclusion to arbitrary spacetime data or claim a constrained exterior extension or global evolution.']
- **LIMIT-14** (approximation): Higher radial decay derivatives of an arbitrary seed are not required by the fourth-order argument, which is applied only after kernel reduction. Blocked use: ['Apply the fourth-order finite coefficient construction directly to arbitrary seeds using unproved higher derivative decay.']
- **LIMIT-15** (hypothesis): The background is the positive-mass Schwarzschild–anti-de Sitter exterior with the specified horizon radius and metric. Blocked use: ['Transfer the conclusion to nonpositive background mass or an unrelated exterior geometry.']
- **LIMIT-16** (hypothesis): The enclosing-area conclusion additionally requires the boundary sign and the enclosing-area hypothesis, including disconnected competitors. Blocked use: ['Identify the boundary area with the enclosing-area infimum using the main theorem alone.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the asymptotically hyperbolic Penrose inequality for sufficiently small maximal vacuum conformal perturbations of a positive-mass Schwarzschild–anti-de Sitter exterior, for every fixed decaying transverse-traceless seed and every solution branch satisfying the stated decay and mass assumptions. The bound uses the area of the marginally outer trapped boundary itself; it requires neither outermostness nor outer area-minimization, and no bulk-versus-boundary domination condition. For sufficiently small positive parameters, equality holds for radial seeds and the inequality is strict otherwise.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

<a id="eq:intro-penrose"></a>
The Penrose inequality relates the total mass of gravitational initial data to the area of a black-hole boundary. Its original formulation arose from the relation between gravitational collapse and cosmic censorship (Penrose 1973) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Penrose1973](#ref-Penrose1973)]. In the asymptotically flat, time-symmetric setting it was proved by Huisken and Ilmanen for a connected horizon and by Bray for the total area of possibly disconnected horizons (Huisken and Ilmanen 2001; Bray 2001) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[HuiskenIlmanen2001](#ref-HuiskenIlmanen2001), [Bray2001](#ref-Bray2001)]. For negative cosmological constant, the Schwarzschild–anti-de Sitter family suggests the three-dimensional inequality $$\label{eq:intro-penrose}
 m_{\mathrm{AH}}\geq
 \sqrt{\frac{A}{16\pi}}\left(1+\frac{A}{4\pi}\right),$$ when the cosmological constant is normalized to $-3$. Here $m_{\mathrm{AH}}$ is the Lorentz norm of the hyperbolic mass covector. In the outermost, outer area-minimizing formulation of Corollary <a href="#cor:penrose" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:penrose">2</a>, $A$ is the least enclosing area of the future marginally outer trapped boundary.

We prove <a href="#eq:intro-penrose" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:intro-penrose">[eq:intro-penrose]</a> for a local conformal class of maximal vacuum data with a marginally outer trapped boundary. The background is a time-symmetric Schwarzschild–anti-de Sitter exterior of arbitrary positive mass. One fixes a smooth decaying transverse-traceless (TT) tensor $q$ and considers any solution branch of the conformal constraint equation with conformal second fundamental form $\epsilon q$. The parameter interval in the result may depend on this fixed seed and branch. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> imposes neither symmetry nor an additional comparison between the size of the seed in the interior and at the boundary.

This setting is closely related to the perturbative theorem of Khuri and Kopiński (Khuri and Kopiński 2023, Theorem 2) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[KhuriKopinski2023](#ref-KhuriKopinski2023)]. Their sufficient condition requires the static-lapse-weighted integral of the squared seed norm to dominate a multiple of the squared $H^1$ norm of its normal component on a domain containing the boundary; see their Equation (2.5). Both sides scale quadratically when the seed is multiplied by $\epsilon$, so shrinking $\epsilon$ alone does not remove that condition. The argument below replaces it by a sharp TT boundary identity and treats the equality directions of the resulting quadratic estimate. It thereby proves the fixed-seed local form of the Penrose inequality in this conformal class without the domination hypothesis. In particular, it applies when the boundary is outermost and outer area-minimizing, as in the usual local Penrose formulation.

Two related geometric ingredients have established precedents. The hyperbolic mass covector and its covariance are studied by Chruściel and Herzlich (Chruściel and Herzlich 2003) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[ChruscielHerzlich2003](#ref-ChruscielHerzlich2003)]. Neves and Tian construct constant mean curvature (CMC) foliations near infinity under positive-mass Schwarzschild–anti-de Sitter asymptotic hypotheses (Neves and Tian 2009) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[NevesTian2009](#ref-NevesTian2009)]; Ambrozio uses CMC foliations to prove a local Riemannian Penrose inequality near Schwarzschild–anti-de Sitter (Ambrozio 2015) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Ambrozio2015](#ref-Ambrozio2015)]. We derive directly the mass and CMC identities needed here. Only finite Taylor families of surfaces are used, so the proof does not require an actual global CMC foliation for a nonzero parameter.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The mechanism of the proof

Write $\mathfrak D_q(\epsilon)$ for the difference between the two sides of <a href="#eq:intro-penrose" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:intro-penrose">[eq:intro-penrose]</a>, with $A$ the boundary area. In the notation of Section <a href="#sec:setting" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:setting">2</a>, $g_0$ is the background metric of mass $m_0$, $s$ is proper radial distance, $r(s)$ is the area radius, and $N=r'(s)$ is the static lapse. The constant and linear deficit coefficients vanish. The quadratic calculation combines a CMC Hawking-mass identity with the static TT identity $$NQ(v)=\mathop{\mathrm{sym}}\nabla(N\,\mathrm{d}v-v\,\mathrm{d}N),
 \qquad Q(v)=\nabla^2v-v(\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}_{g_0}+3g_0).$$ After the boundary area correction, the quadratic coefficient is a sum of nonnegative terms (Proposition <a href="#prop:quadratic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:quadratic">15</a>). Its kernel is the four-dimensional space of tensors $Q(v)$, where $(\Delta_{g_0}-3)v=0$, $v$ decays, and $v|_S$ contains only constant and degree-one spherical harmonics (Corollary <a href="#cor:kernel" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:kernel">16</a>).

The degree-one directions are a real obstruction to concluding from second order. They have zero first-order shear and lapse fluctuation, and a radial component can make their boundary normal component nonnegative. We remove their first extrinsic-curvature coefficient by a change of spacetime slice at the level of finite Taylor expansions. The resulting quartic coefficient is again a sum of nonnegative terms. If it vanishes, the changed metric must be a round warped-product jet through second order. Conformality of the original branch then forces the radial profile $V(s)$ of the degree-one part of $v$ to satisfy (with primes denoting $s$-derivatives) $$\left(V'-\frac{r'}rV\right)^2=\frac{6m_0}{r^3}V^2.$$ This is incompatible with a nonzero decaying profile. Thus the quartic coefficient is strictly positive in every nonradial quadratic-null direction (Proposition <a href="#prop:quartic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:quartic">18</a>). The remaining radial directions satisfy an exact conserved-mass identity (Proposition <a href="#prop:radial" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:radial">23</a>).

Three parts of the method may be useful beyond this application: the sharp static TT boundary square; the passage from CMC surface jets to a positive mass-coefficient identity by integrating first at finite radius; and the use of a null boundary transport together with a change of slice to detect a quartic obstruction. Analytically, an exact Green formula converts the derivative-defined mass into convergent integrals. It allows actual remainder estimates in a weighted function norm, and rotation averaging then covers seeds with arbitrary angular dependence. Proposition <a href="#prop:expansions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:expansions">8</a> then identifies the finite Taylor calculations with the actual deficit.
